Title: The Works of the Reverend George Whitefield, M.A., late of Pembroke College, Oxford, and Chaplain to the Rt. Hon. the Countess of Huntingdon; containing all his sermons and tracts, with a select collection of letters written to his most intimate friends and persons of distinction in England, Scotland, Ireland and America, from the year 1734 to 1770; Volume I (letters)
Creator: George Whitefield (1714-1770); edited by John Gillies
Publisher: London: printed for Edward and Charles Dilly in the Poultry, and Messrs Kincaid and Bell at Edinburgh
Publication Date: 1771
Rights: Public Domain (published 1771, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item's own rights statement reads 'NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT')

Content note: The first volume of Whitefield's Works: his letters from 1734 onward, numbered and dated, to friends, converts and patrons across the Atlantic world; the revivalist's correspondence as his executors collected it.

ADVERTISEMENT. 


HIS complete Edition of the late Rev. Mr, 
WuitTEFreLD’s Works (printed under the 
Pireétion of his Executors) it is prefumed, will be 
highly acceptable to all his Friends, as the juft and 
proper Monument of his Memory and Merit; and 
both pleafing and ufeful to the Public in general, but 
efpecially to thofe who defire to cherifh and promote 
the Spirit of primitive Chriftianity, 


To the Sermons and Trats formerly publifhed, and 
which are now ranged in their proper Order, are alfa 
added other Pieces, on the moft Important Subjects ; 
together with a valuable Collection of Letters, felected 
and prepared by himfelf for Publication; in which is 
difplayed, that native Spirit and Simplicity, fo emt- 
nently confpicuous in his Life and Converfation. His 
Friends, and even his Enemies (fhould there be any 
fuch) will here openly behold his unwearied Diligence, 
undaunted Firmnefs, noble Difintereftednefs, and ex- 
ceeding Ufefulnefs in the Work of the Miniftry; alfo, 
his remarkable Fidelity in Friendfhip, exemplary 
Piety, and fervent Zeal for the Profperity of pure and 
undefiled Religion. 


The Letters and Works can ftand in no need of any 
Recoggymendation : Connected with the account of his 
Life, (now drawn from original Papers) they exhibit 
a plain and undifguifed View of the worthy Author, in 
all Parts of his public Service, as well as in his private 
Retirements, and inward Trials; faithfully fhewing 
the Whole of that ving Temple, which was facred to 
God, and happily.inftruéting Mankind in the Ways of 
Godlinefs and eternal Life,

Dear Sir, Oxon, Fuly 18, 1734.

| To the Allegany Indians. 

My Dear Brethren, _ Reedy Iftand, May 21, 1740. 
Ti\OR fo I call you, becaufe you, and I, and all, have one. 
common father, OnE Gop, in whom we live, move and 
have qur beings We all] came at firft out of the duft of the 
earth. For after Gop made the fun, moon, ftars, and every 
thing you fee, he alfo made a creature called man, and breathed 
-into him the breath of life; he then became a living foul. 
He could not only walk and fpeak, but he could alfo think, 
and reafon, and was full of goodnefs, peace, and joy. This 
man, this happy creatare Gop placed in a garden, which his 
own right hand had planted. He then caft him into a deep 
fleep, and out of one of his ribs made what we call a woman, 
that fhe might be his wife. Their names were Adam and 
Eve. With thefe, Gop made an agteement that they fhould

Arrived here laft Saturday evening from the We, where I
